Turning the Spell Around: Lightweight Alignment Amplification via Rank-One Safety Injection
Authors: Harethah Abu Shairah, Hasan Abed Al Kader Hammoud, George Turkiyyah, Bernard Ghanem
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2025-08-28T13:22:33+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T07:43:32+00:00
Comments: EMNLP 2026, Main Conference
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment in Large Language Models (LLMs) often involves mediating internal representations to refuse harmful requests. Recent research has demonstrated that these safety mechanisms can be bypassed by ablating or removing specific representational directions within the model. In this paper, we propose the opposite approach: Rank-One Safety Injection (ROSI), a white-box method that amplifies a model's safety alignment by permanently steering its activations toward the refusal-mediating subspace. ROSI operates as a simple, fine-tuning-free rank-one weight modification applied to all residual stream write matrices. The required safety direction can be computed from a small set of harmful and harmless instruction pairs. We show that ROSI consistently increases safety refusal rates - as evaluated by Llama Guard 3 - while preserving the utility of the model on standard benchmarks such as MMLU, HellaSwag, and Arc. Furthermore, we show that ROSI can also re-align 'uncensored' models by amplifying their own latent safety directions, demonstrating its utility as an effective last-mile safety procedure. Our results suggest that targeted, interpretable weight steering is a cheap and potent mechanism to improve LLM safety, complementing more resource-intensive fine-tuning paradigms.
中文标题/摘要
标题：扭转咒语：通过秩一安全注入实现轻量级对齐放大
大型语言模型（LLMs）中的安全对齐通常涉及调解内部表示以拒绝有害请求。最近的研究表明，这些安全机制可以通过消除或移除模型内特定的表示方向来绕过。在本文中，我们提出了相反的方法：秩一安全注入（ROSI），这是一种白盒方法，通过永久性地将模型的激活引导到拒绝调解子空间来放大模型的安全对齐。ROSI作为一种简单的、无需微调的秩一权重修改，应用于所有残差流写矩阵。所需的安全方向可以从一小组有害和无害的指令对中计算得出。我们展示了ROSI在Llama Guard 3的评估中始终提高了安全拒绝率，同时在MMLU、HellaSwag和Arc等标准基准上保持了模型的效用。此外，我们还展示了ROSI可以通过放大自身潜在安全方向来重新对齐“未审查”模型，证明其作为有效的最后一公里安全程序的实用性。我们的结果表明，针对性的、可解释的权重引导是一种廉价而有效的机制，可以提高LLM的安全性，补充更资源密集的微调范式。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of safety alignment in Large Language Models (LLMs), which can be compromised by removing specific representational directions. Previous methods often involve complex fine-tuning processes that may not effectively enhance safety mechanisms. The proposed Rank-One Safety Injection (ROSI) method diverges from these approaches by applying a straightforward, fine-tuning-free rank-one weight modification to enhance safety alignment without sacrificing model utility. This method computes the necessary safety direction from a limited set of harmful and harmless instruction pairs and has been shown to significantly improve safety refusal rates, as evaluated by Llama Guard 3, while maintaining performance on standard benchmarks like MMLU, HellaSwag, and Arc. The findings indicate that ROSI serves as an efficient and interpretable mechanism for improving LLM safety, providing a viable alternative to more resource-intensive methods.
本研究解决了大型语言模型（LLMs）中安全对齐的挑战，现有方法常常因有害请求能够通过改变特定内部表示而绕过安全机制而失效。提出的秩一安全注入（ROSI）方法与过去的方法不同，它通过增强安全对齐而不是仅仅调解它，使用简单的秩一权重修改将模型激活引导到拒绝调解子空间。该方法的动机明确，因为它在不需要大量微调的情况下增强了安全性。论文的贡献在于证明ROSI显著提高了安全拒绝率（通过Llama Guard 3测量），同时在标准基准（如MMLU、HellaSwag和Arc）上保持模型性能，从而有效支持其提高LLM安全性的目标。
Single Canonical Prompts Underestimate LLM Safety's Surface-Form Sensitivity
Authors: Yongxi Zhou, Junwei Yao, Yuanzhe Liu, Zihan Dong, Wenbo Ye, Jiaxi Wen, Lai Yun Choi
First: 2026-08-01T22:28:31+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-30T05:09:19+00:00
Comments: Accepted at the Sci-FM Workshop @ COLM 2026 (non-archival). Workshop version with reviews: https://openreview.net/forum?id=mZh0MqpOOC
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
A benchmark score is a measurement instrument, yet most benchmarks read each item at a single canonical surface form. We ask whether that reading is faithful: when an item's intent is held fixed and only its meaning-preserving surface form varies, does the canonical-form score estimate model behavior well, and how much of any variation is decoding/judge noise rather than signal? We instantiate this in safety, a high-stakes setting with no gold label to average toward. To avoid prior confounds, we pre-author the reformulations (refusal-free, mostly non-LLM: machine back-translation and a Matrix-Language-Frame code-switch generator) so an identical surface form reaches every model, score all responses with one human-anchored, vendor-neutral judge (Claude, kappa = 0.86 vs. human on unsafe compliance, stable across languages, cross-checked by GPT-4o), and verify intent preservation. On 370 seeds x 5 surface forms x 5 models, no single transformation is uniformly most dangerous (6 of 20 per-transformation McNemar tests survive correction, most protective). Yet evaluating only the canonical prompt underestimates unsafe compliance: the union of unsafe outcomes across forms exceeds even the worst single form by 3.3-12.9 pp, with bootstrap 95% CIs excluding zero for all five models, and 5-13% of seeds safe on canonical are unsafe under some reformulation -- above a zero stochasticity floor (canonical resampled five times at temperature 0 gives 0/370 new exposures). The size of this gap is model-dependent (largest on Gemini 2.5 Pro). One form recovers only ~53% of a model's observed unsafe surface and about three reach 85% -- a redundancy characterization of this form set, not of a defined population. A benign control (XSTest) suggests the instability is bidirectional, though the benign and harmful pools are not item-matched. We release the dataset, code, and per-response labels.
中文标题/摘要
标题：单一规范提示低估了大型语言模型安全性的表面形式敏感性
基准分数是一种测量工具，但大多数基准以单一规范表面形式读取每个项目。我们询问这种读取是否忠实：当项目的意图保持不变，仅其保持意义的表面形式变化时，规范形式分数是否能很好地估计模型行为，以及任何变化中有多少是解码/评判噪声而非信号？我们在安全性这一高风险环境中实例化这一点，因其没有金标准可供平均。为了避免先前的混淆，我们预先授权了重构（无拒绝，主要非大型语言模型：机器回译和矩阵语言框架代码切换生成器），以便相同的表面形式到达每个模型，所有响应由一个人类锚定的、中立的评审（Claude，kappa = 0.86与人类在不安全合规性上的比较，跨语言稳定，由GPT-4o交叉检查）进行评分，并验证意图保持。在370个种子x 5个表面形式x 5个模型中，没有单一变换是普遍最危险的（20个每变换的McNemar测试中有6个在校正后存活，大多数是保护性的）。然而，仅评估规范提示低估了不安全合规性：跨形式的不安全结果的并集甚至超过了最糟糕的单一形式3.3-12.9个百分点，所有五个模型的自助法95%置信区间均不包括零，且5-13%的在规范下安全的种子在某些重构下是不安全的——高于零随机性底线（规范在温度0下重采样五次给出0/370个新暴露）。这一差距的大小依赖于模型（在Gemini 2.5 Pro上最大）。一种形式仅恢复了模型观察到的不安全表面的约53%，大约三种形式达到了85%——这是对这一形式集的冗余特征，而非定义人口的特征。一个良性对照（XSTest）表明不稳定性是双向的，尽管良性和有害池并未按项目匹配。我们发布了数据集、代码和每个响应的标签。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of existing benchmark scores in evaluating large language models (LLMs) by questioning the fidelity of single canonical surface forms in estimating model behavior, particularly in safety contexts where no gold standard exists. Previous methods often relied on a single transformation, which may not capture the full range of model responses, leading to an underestimation of unsafe compliance. The proposed approach involves pre-authoring multiple reformulations of prompts to ensure consistent surface forms across models, utilizing a human-anchored judge for scoring, and verifying intent preservation. The study's methodology includes testing 370 seeds across five surface forms and five models, revealing that evaluating only the canonical prompt significantly underestimates unsafe outcomes, with the union of unsafe responses across forms exceeding the worst single form by 3.3-12.9 percentage points. This indicates that the proposed method effectively highlights the variability in model responses and contributes to a better understanding of LLM safety. The findings suggest that a single form captures only a fraction of a model's unsafe behavior, emphasizing the need for diverse prompt evaluations in safety assessments.
本研究针对现有基准评分在评估大型语言模型（LLMs）时的局限性，尤其是在没有金标准的安全评估中。以往的方法通常依赖于单一的规范表面形式，这可能无法准确反映模型行为，因为解码噪声的变化。所提出的方法涉及预先编写多个提示的改写，以确保在模型之间表面形式的一致性，并通过人类锚定的评判来评估响应，以保持意图的保留。研究发现，仅依赖规范提示显著低估了不安全合规性，揭示了不同形式的不安全结果的联合可以超过最糟糕的单一形式，且差距显著。该方法论包括在五个模型上测试370个种子和五个表面形式，表明没有单一的转化形式是普遍最危险的，并强调了安全评估的模型依赖性。研究结果支持对LLM安全性进行更细致的评估，作者还提供了他们的数据集和代码以供进一步研究。
NeuronFuzz: Safety Neuron Guided Fuzzing for LLM Safety Evaluation
Authors: Zhiyuan Xu, Muhammad Firhard Roslan, Joseph Gardiner, Sana Belguith, Lichao Wu
First: 2026-08-26T12:44:32+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-26T12:44:32+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety evaluation is critical for assessing whether aligned Large Language Models (LLMs) remain robust against jailbreak attacks. Existing automated testing methods, however, largely rely on response-level feedback: each candidate prompt typically requires generating a target-model response to evaluate its attack effectiveness. This process is expensive and, more importantly, provides only sparse guidance on strongly aligned models, where most candidates are rejected with the same failure outcome. This paper presents NeuronFuzz, a white-box fuzzing framework that exploits internal safety neurons as continuous execution feedback for LLM safety evaluation. A SafetyOracle converts safety-neuron activations into a continuous safety alarm score that serves as feedback for fuzzing and can be obtained during prefill, eliminating response generation from the fuzzing loop. To construct the SafetyOracle, NeuronFuzz uses template-invariant harmful and benign inputs and stability-aware selection to identify a compact set of safety neurons whose activations capture harmful-intent recognition. Moreover, since the safety alarm score is differentiable, NeuronFuzz uses its gradients to identify safety-sensitive template positions and a masked language model to generate fluent, context-compatible mutations while preserving original harmful payload and avoiding additional optimization variables. We evaluate NeuronFuzz across 21 text and multimodal models. Across five white-box source models, it achieves a 76-100% jailbreak discovery rate, outperforming baselines by up to 48 percentage points. Its optimized templates further transfer zero-shot to open-weight and six proprietary target models, achieving average ASR and top-5 ensemble ASR (EASR) of 69.6%/92.6% and 44.1%/60.0%, respectively.
中文标题/摘要
标题：NeuronFuzz：安全神经元引导的LLM安全评估模糊测试
安全评估对于评估对齐的大型语言模型（LLM）是否能够抵御越狱攻击至关重要。然而，现有的自动化测试方法主要依赖于响应级反馈：每个候选提示通常需要生成目标模型的响应以评估其攻击有效性。这个过程成本高昂，更重要的是，对于大多数候选者在强对齐模型中被拒绝的情况下，仅提供稀疏的指导。 本文提出了NeuronFuzz，一个利用内部安全神经元作为LLM安全评估的连续执行反馈的白盒模糊测试框架。SafetyOracle将安全神经元的激活转换为连续的安全警报分数，作为模糊测试的反馈，并且可以在预填充期间获得，从而消除模糊测试循环中的响应生成。为了构建SafetyOracle，NeuronFuzz使用模板不变的有害和良性输入以及稳定性感知选择，识别出一组紧凑的安全神经元，其激活捕捉有害意图识别。此外，由于安全警报分数是可微分的，NeuronFuzz利用其梯度识别安全敏感的模板位置，并使用掩码语言模型生成流畅、上下文兼容的变异，同时保留原始有害负载并避免额外的优化变量。我们在21个文本和多模态模型上评估了NeuronFuzz。在五个白盒源模型中，它实现了76-100%的越狱发现率，超越基线最多48个百分点。其优化模板进一步零-shot转移到开放权重和六个专有目标模型，分别实现平均ASR和前5个集成ASR（EASR）为69.6%/92.6%和44.1%/60.0%。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for effective safety evaluation of aligned Large Language Models (LLMs) against jailbreak attacks, highlighting limitations in existing automated testing methods that rely on response-level feedback, which is costly and offers limited guidance. The proposed NeuronFuzz framework differs by utilizing internal safety neurons to provide continuous execution feedback, thereby eliminating the need for response generation in the fuzzing process. This approach is well-motivated as it aims to enhance the robustness of LLMs against harmful inputs. NeuronFuzz constructs a SafetyOracle that translates safety-neuron activations into a continuous safety alarm score, facilitating the identification of safety-sensitive template positions and generating context-compatible mutations. The methodology was evaluated on 21 text and multimodal models, achieving a jailbreak discovery rate of 76-100% across five white-box source models, significantly outperforming existing methods by up to 48 percentage points, thus supporting its effectiveness in enhancing LLM safety evaluation.
本研究解决了对齐的大型语言模型（LLM）在针对越狱攻击时进行有效安全评估的迫切需求，强调了现有依赖响应级反馈的自动化测试方法的局限性，这些方法成本高且提供的指导有限。提出的NeuronFuzz框架通过利用内部安全神经元提供连续执行反馈，从而消除了在模糊测试过程中生成响应的需要，这一方法与现有方法有所不同，且具有良好的动机，提升了安全评估的效率和有效性。NeuronFuzz构建了一个SafetyOracle，将安全神经元的激活转换为连续的安全警报分数，从而能够识别安全敏感的模板位置并生成上下文兼容的变异。该方法在21个文本和多模态模型上进行了评估，取得了76-100%的越狱发现率，并且在性能上比现有方法提高了多达48个百分点，证明了其在增强LLM安全评估方面的有效性。
ADVERSA: Measuring Multi-Turn Guardrail Degradation and Judge Reliability in Large Language Models
Authors: Harry Owiredu-Ashley
Venue: 2026 IEEE/ACIS 24th International Conference on Software Engineering Research, Management and Applications (SERA), Towson, MD, USA, 2026, pp. 414-417
First: 2026-03-10T03:00:34+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-25T05:47:14+00:00
Comments: 12 pages, 12 figures. Independent research. Code and artifacts: https://github.com/Harry-Ashley/adversa-guardrail-degradation. Published in the 2026 IEEE/ACIS 24th International Conference on Software Engineering Research, Management and Applications (SERA), pp. 414-417. Recipient of the Best Paper Award at IEEE/ACIS SERA 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Most adversarial evaluations of large language model (LLM) safety assess single prompts and report binary pass/fail outcomes, which fails to capture how safety properties evolve under sustained adversarial interaction. We present ADVERSA, an automated red-teaming framework that measures guardrail degradation dynamics as continuous per-round compliance trajectories rather than discrete jailbreak events. ADVERSA uses a fine-tuned 70B attacker model (ADVERSA-Red, Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct with QLoRA) that eliminates the attacker-side safety refusals that render off-the-shelf models unreliable as attackers, scoring victim responses on a structured 5-point rubric that treats partial compliance as a distinct measurable state. We report a controlled experiment across three frontier victim models (Claude Opus 4.6, Gemini 3.1 Pro, GPT-5.2) using a triple-judge consensus architecture in which judge reliability is measured as a first-class research outcome rather than assumed. Across 15 conversations of up to 10 adversarial rounds, we observe a 26.7% jailbreak rate with an average jailbreak round of 1.25, suggesting that in this evaluation setting, successful jailbreaks were concentrated in early rounds rather than accumulating through sustained pressure. We document inter-judge agreement rates, self-judge scoring tendencies, attacker drift as a failure mode in fine-tuned attackers deployed out of their training distribution, and attacker refusals as a previously-underreported confound in victim resistance measurement. All limitations are stated explicitly. Attack prompts are withheld per responsible disclosure policy; all other experimental artifacts are released.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ADVERSA：测量大型语言模型中的多轮护栏退化和评审可靠性
大多数针对大型语言模型（LLM）安全性的对抗性评估评估单一提示并报告二元通过/失败结果，这未能捕捉安全属性在持续对抗交互下的演变。我们提出了ADVERSA，一个自动化红队框架，测量护栏退化动态，作为连续的每轮合规轨迹，而不是离散的越狱事件。ADVERSA使用经过微调的70B攻击者模型（ADVERSA-Red，Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct与QLoRA），消除了攻击者侧的安全拒绝，使现成模型在攻击者中不可靠，使用结构化的5分制评分受害者响应，将部分合规视为一个独特的可测量状态。我们报告了在三个前沿受害者模型（Claude Opus 4.6，Gemini 3.1 Pro，GPT-5.2）中进行的受控实验，使用三重评审共识架构，其中评审可靠性被视为一项一流的研究结果，而不是假设。在15次对话中，最多进行10轮对抗，我们观察到26.7%的越狱率，平均越狱轮次为1.25，表明在该评估环境中，成功的越狱集中在早期轮次，而不是通过持续压力积累。我们记录了评审间一致性率、自我评审评分倾向、在训练分布外部署的微调攻击者中的攻击者漂移作为失败模式，以及攻击者拒绝作为受害者抵抗测量中之前未报告的混淆因素。所有限制均明确说明。攻击提示根据负责任的披露政策被保留；所有其他实验材料均已发布。
Summary / 总结
This research addresses the limitations of existing adversarial evaluations of large language models (LLMs), which typically assess safety through single prompts and binary outcomes, failing to capture the dynamics of safety properties during prolonged adversarial interactions. The proposed ADVERSA framework offers a novel approach by measuring guardrail degradation as continuous compliance trajectories rather than discrete events, utilizing a fine-tuned 70B attacker model to enhance reliability in adversarial assessments. The paper contributes to the field by introducing a triple-judge consensus architecture that quantifies judge reliability as a key outcome, revealing a 26.7% jailbreak rate across three leading victim models over 15 conversations with up to 10 adversarial rounds. This methodology not only highlights the concentration of successful jailbreaks in early rounds but also documents various failure modes and confounding factors, thus providing a more nuanced understanding of model vulnerabilities.
本研究解决了现有大型语言模型（LLM）对抗性评估的局限性，这些评估通常通过单一提示和二元结果来评估安全性，未能捕捉到在持续对抗交互中安全属性的动态变化。提出的ADVERSA框架引入了一种新颖的自动化红队方法，将护栏降解测量为连续的合规轨迹，而非离散事件，利用经过微调的70B攻击者模型来提高对受害者响应评分的可靠性。该研究的贡献在于采用三重评审共识架构，将评审者可靠性作为关键结果进行评估，揭示在15次对话中有26.7%的越狱率，平均每轮越狱次数为1.25，表明成功的越狱更可能发生在早期交互中。这种方法论提供了对模型脆弱性和评审者表现的更细致理解，支持了改善LLM安全评估的目标。
NeuronGuard: Robust LLM Safety Alignment via Ablation-Aware Safety Signal Redistribution
Authors: Anjun Gao, Yueyang Quan, Yufei Xia, Zhuqing Liu, Minghong Fang
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-08-25T01:36:54+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-25T01:36:54+00:00
Comments: To appear in EMNLP 2026 (Findings)
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment in large language models (LLMs) remains brittle against a growing spectrum of attacks. Jailbreak attacks bypass safety mechanisms through crafted prompts, while neuron-level attacks directly prune safety-critical neurons post-deployment. Both exploit a common weakness: safety-relevant information concentrates in a sparse neuron subset. We present NeuronGuard, a fine-tuning-stage defense that simultaneously hardens LLMs against both attack classes by redistributing safety signals across a broader set of neurons. NeuronGuard dynamically identifies safety-critical neurons via periodically refreshed per-layer linear classifiers, forces refusal behavior under deliberate neuron ablation, and applies KL-divergence regularization for distributional consistency. A randomized gradient projection strategy preserves downstream task utility by resolving conflicts between the defense and task objectives. We provide a formal guarantee that NeuronGuard strictly reduces the attack success rate (ASR) upper bound, and experiments across three LLMs, six state-of-the-art attack strategies, and multimodal settings confirm near-zero ASR while maintaining task accuracy, including against white-box adaptive adversaries.
中文标题/摘要
标题：NeuronGuard：通过消融感知安全信号重分配实现稳健的LLM安全对齐
大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全对齐在面对日益增长的攻击谱时仍然脆弱。越狱攻击通过精心设计的提示绕过安全机制，而神经元级攻击则在部署后直接修剪安全关键神经元。两者都利用了一个共同的弱点：与安全相关的信息集中在稀疏的神经元子集中。我们提出了NeuronGuard，这是一种微调阶段的防御方法，通过在更广泛的神经元集上重新分配安全信号，来同时增强LLMs对这两类攻击的抵御能力。NeuronGuard通过定期更新的每层线性分类器动态识别安全关键神经元，在故意消融神经元时强制拒绝行为，并应用KL散度正则化以保持分布一致性。一种随机梯度投影策略通过解决防御与任务目标之间的冲突，保持下游任务的效用。我们提供了一个正式保证，NeuronGuard严格降低了攻击成功率（ASR）的上限，且在三种LLM、六种最先进的攻击策略和多模态设置下的实验确认了接近零的ASR，同时保持任务准确性，包括对抗白盒自适应对手的情况。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to various safety attacks, specifically jailbreak and neuron-level attacks, which exploit the concentration of safety-relevant information in a limited number of neurons. Previous methods have struggled to provide robust defenses against these attacks, often failing to maintain task performance while enhancing safety. The proposed NeuronGuard approach differs by redistributing safety signals across a wider neuron set, dynamically identifying critical neurons, and employing techniques such as KL-divergence regularization and randomized gradient projection to balance safety and task objectives. This paper contributes a novel fine-tuning defense mechanism that guarantees a reduction in attack success rates while preserving task accuracy. Experimental results demonstrate that NeuronGuard achieves near-zero attack success rates across multiple LLMs and attack strategies, effectively supporting its safety alignment goals without compromising performance.
本研究针对大型语言模型（LLMs）在面对各种安全攻击时的脆弱性，特别是越狱攻击和神经元级攻击，这些攻击利用了安全信息集中在有限神经元中的弱点。以往的方法在提供对这些攻击类型的强大防御方面存在困难，因此需要一种更全面的方法。所提出的方法NeuronGuard通过在更广泛的神经元中重新分配安全信号来增强安全对齐，动态识别关键神经元，并采用KL散度正则化等技术来保持性能。本文贡献了一种新颖的微调防御机制，显著降低了攻击成功率，同时保持任务准确性，实验结果表明在多种LLM和攻击策略下，攻击成功率接近零，证明了其有效性。
Register Shifts Break LLM Safety: A Bengali Benchmark with Culturally Grounded Harms
Authors: Naymul Islam, Nusrat Jahan Lia, Shubhashis Roy Dipta, Sabik Bin Sultan, Abdullah Khan Zehady
First: 2026-08-23T10:09:00+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-23T10:09:00+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Bengali is the seventh-most-spoken language globally, yet LLM safety evaluation remains overwhelmingly English-centric. We introduce BanglaSafe, a benchmark of 879 Bengali prompts combining 309 natively authored prompts with 570 expert-reviewed prompts, spanning 17 culturally grounded harm categories and five prompting conditions that vary language, writing style, and authority framing. Evaluating 18 frontier LLMs, we find that over half of all responses are unsafe or partially unsafe (53.6%) while 14.7% contains strictly harmful content, and that the strongest observed effect is not the switch from English to Bengali but the choice of writing style within Bengali: the same harmful request phrased as a formal newspaper investigation succeeds 17 percentage points more often than the same request phrased as a casual message, with no adversarial engineering involved. We further show that existing safety classifiers struggle to reliably evaluate Bengali content, with even frontier models failing on nearly half of all cases.
中文标题/摘要
标题：注册班次突破LLM安全：一个具有文化基础危害的孟加拉基准
孟加拉语是全球第七大语言，但LLM安全评估仍然以英语为中心。我们推出了BanglaSafe，这是一个包含879个孟加拉提示的基准，结合了309个本土创作的提示和570个专家审查的提示，涵盖17个文化基础的危害类别和五种变化语言、写作风格和权威框架的提示条件。评估18个前沿LLM，我们发现超过一半的响应是不安全或部分不安全的（53.6%），而14.7%包含严格有害内容，观察到的最强效应不是从英语切换到孟加拉语，而是在孟加拉语中选择写作风格：同样的有害请求以正式的报纸调查形式表达时成功率比以随意消息形式表达时高出17个百分点，且没有涉及对抗性工程。我们进一步表明，现有的安全分类器在可靠评估孟加拉内容方面存在困难，甚至前沿模型在近一半的案例中也未能成功。

Redteaming Leading Arabic LLMs with ASAS
Authors: Fidaa Abed, Haidar Khan, M Saiful Bari, Babar Khan, Abdalghani Abujabal
First: 2026-08-22T14:34:08+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-22T14:34:08+00:00
Comments: 13 pages, 5 figues, 3 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
As the adoption of large language models (LLMs) grows in Arabic-speaking regions, ensuring their safety and cultural alignment is increasingly critical. However, Arabic LLM safety remains underexplored, especially in adversarial evaluation settings. We introduce the Arabic Safety Index (ASAS), the first fully human-curated Arabic benchmark for redteaming LLMs. ASAS contains 801 prompts spanning 8 safety categories and 8 attack strategies, with ideal responses in Modern Standard Arabic (MSA). We conduct a redteaming evaluation across seven leading models with Arabic capabilities, including GPT-4o, Claude 3.7 Sonnet, and regional models such as ALLaM and FANAR. Human annotators rate responses using a structured 4-point safety scale, revealing that most models fail to defend against 50% of unsafe prompts. Our findings highlight major safety gaps in high-harm categories such as weapons and illicit substances, with direct and obfuscation-based attacks proving most effective. The results also show that language alignment does not readily transfer across languages, and that automated safety judges (e.g., GPT-4o) perform poorly compared to human annotators. ASAS provides a culturally grounded benchmark and redteaming protocol to drive progress in Arabic LLM safety.
中文标题/摘要
标题：使用ASAS对领先的阿拉伯语LLM进行红队测试
随着大型语言模型（LLMs）在阿拉伯语地区的普及，确保其安全性和文化一致性变得愈发重要。然而，阿拉伯语LLM的安全性仍然未得到充分探索，尤其是在对抗性评估环境中。我们引入了阿拉伯安全指数（ASAS），这是第一个完全由人工策划的阿拉伯基准，用于对LLMs进行红队测试。ASAS包含801个提示，涵盖8个安全类别和8种攻击策略，并提供现代标准阿拉伯语（MSA）的理想响应。我们对七个具有阿拉伯语能力的领先模型进行了红队评估，包括GPT-4o、Claude 3.7 Sonnet以及区域模型如ALLaM和FANAR。人类注释者使用结构化的4分安全评分标准对响应进行评分，结果显示大多数模型未能抵御50%的不安全提示。我们的发现突显了在武器和非法物质等高危类别中的主要安全缺口，直接攻击和混淆攻击被证明最为有效。结果还表明，语言一致性并不能轻易跨语言转移，自动安全评估者（例如GPT-4o）的表现远不及人类注释者。ASAS提供了一个文化基础的基准和红队测试协议，以推动阿拉伯语LLM安全性的进展。

CLEAR: Continuous Latent Adapter Routing for Utility-Preserving LLM Safety Alignment
Authors: Chengxiao Wang, Enyi Jiang, Xiaojing Liao, Sanmi Koyejo
First: 2026-08-21T16:36:10+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T16:36:10+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Improving the safety of large language models (LLMs) often comes at the expense of utility, as globally applied safety tuning may affect model responses to both harmful and benign inputs. We propose \textbf{C}ontinuous \textbf{L}at\textbf{E}nt \textbf{A}dapter \textbf{R}outing (CLEAR), a conditional safety adaptation framework that uses a lightweight hidden-state gate to continuously control the activation strength of a safety low-rank adapter. CLEAR aims to reduce harmful completions while avoiding unnecessary changes to the frozen backbone that could degrade performance on benign prompts. Experiments on widely used safety and utility benchmarks show that CLEAR improves robustness on HarmBench while reducing the utility degradation observed with globally applied safety tuning such as SFT or standard low-rank adaptation (LoRA). On Llama-3-8B-Instruct, CLEAR reduces HarmBench ASR from 32.3\% to 0.5\%, while retaining most of the base model's utility and achieving up to 7.1 percentage points higher GSM8K accuracy than globally applied SFT or LoRA. These results suggest that CLEAR is a promising mechanism for improving the safety--utility trade-off in LLM alignment.
中文标题/摘要
标题：CLEAR：用于保持效用的LLM安全对齐的连续潜在适配器路由
提高大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全性往往会以效用为代价，因为全局应用的安全调优可能会影响模型对有害和良性输入的响应。我们提出了\textbf{C}ontinuous \textbf{L}at\textbf{E}nt \textbf{A}dapter \textbf{R}outing（CLEAR），这是一种条件安全适应框架，使用轻量级的隐藏状态门控来持续控制安全低秩适配器的激活强度。CLEAR旨在减少有害的完成，同时避免对冻结主干的不必要更改，这可能会降低对良性提示的性能。对广泛使用的安全和效用基准的实验表明，CLEAR在HarmBench上提高了鲁棒性，同时减少了全局应用安全调优（如SFT或标准低秩适配（LoRA））所观察到的效用下降。在Llama-3-8B-Instruct上，CLEAR将HarmBench ASR从32.3\%降低到0.5\%，同时保留了大部分基础模型的效用，并在GSM8K准确率上比全局应用的SFT或LoRA高出多达7.1个百分点。这些结果表明，CLEAR是改善LLM对齐中安全与效用权衡的有希望的机制。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of enhancing the safety of large language models (LLMs) without compromising their utility, as traditional methods like globally applied safety tuning often degrade model performance on benign inputs. The proposed Continuous Latent Adapter Routing (CLEAR) framework introduces a lightweight hidden-state gate that allows for continuous control over the activation strength of a safety low-rank adapter, effectively mitigating harmful outputs while preserving the model's utility. This approach is well-motivated as it seeks to balance safety and utility, which has been a significant issue in previous methods. The methodology involves applying CLEAR to safety and utility benchmarks, demonstrating its effectiveness in reducing harmful completions on HarmBench while maintaining performance on benign prompts. The results indicate that CLEAR significantly lowers the HarmBench ASR from 32.3% to 0.5% on Llama-3-8B-Instruct and achieves up to 7.1 percentage points higher accuracy on GSM8K compared to traditional safety tuning methods, thus supporting its goal of improving the safety-utility trade-off in LLM alignment.

RouteScan: A Non-Intrusive Approach to Auditing MoE LLMs Safety via Expert Routing Telemetry
Authors: Bo Lv, Zhiheng Xu, KeDong Xiu, Ruyi Ding, Tianhang Zheng, Zhibo Wang, Kui Ren
First: 2026-05-24T02:06:44+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T12:55:57+00:00
Comments: 11 pages. Revised manuscript with expanded experiments
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
As Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures are increasingly adopted for scaling Large Language Models (LLMs), safety auditing becomes necessary to verify whether these models produce or facilitate harmful behaviors during operation. However, existing content-based auditing methods typically require access to user prompts, model internals, or outputs, potentially exposing sensitive user information and creating a tension between LLM safety and user privacy. On the other hand, we observe that, in MoE models, different inputs induce different sparse expert-routing patterns, which produce measurable footprints in low-level GPU execution telemetry. We refer to these hardware-observable signals induced by expert-routing decisions as expert routing telemetry; they are derived from GPU execution rather than from router logits or token-level routing assignments. Inspired by this observation, we propose RouteScan, a non-intrusive auditing framework for detecting harmful behaviors through such routing-induced GPU telemetry. Specifically, RouteScan utilizes the number of active GPU threads allocated to expert modules during the prefilling phase as a discriminative micro-architectural fingerprint, and builds a lightweight detection pipeline that isolates cross-domain invariant risk indicators for the precise identification of malicious prompts. Comprehensive evaluations on four open-source MoE LLMs with distinct routing designs demonstrate that RouteScan achieves strong generalization, with an AUROC exceeding 0.91 on unseen harmful domains. Moreover, privacy stress tests show that, although aggregated execution telemetry retains input-related attribute information, full prompts and exact sensitive fields cannot be reliably recovered under the evaluated attacks.
中文标题/摘要
标题：RouteScan：一种通过专家路由遥测对MoE LLM安全进行非侵入性审计的方法
随着混合专家（MoE）架构在大规模语言模型（LLM）中的日益采用，安全审计变得必要，以验证这些模型在操作过程中是否产生或促进有害行为。然而，现有的基于内容的审计方法通常需要访问用户提示、模型内部或输出，可能会暴露敏感用户信息，并在LLM安全与用户隐私之间产生紧张关系。另一方面，我们观察到，在MoE模型中，不同的输入会引发不同的稀疏专家路由模式，这在低级GPU执行遥测中产生可测量的足迹。我们将这些由专家路由决策引发的硬件可观察信号称为专家路由遥测；它们源自GPU执行，而不是来自路由器logits或令牌级路由分配。受此观察的启发，我们提出了RouteScan，一种通过这种路由引发的GPU遥测检测有害行为的非侵入性审计框架。具体而言，RouteScan利用在预填充阶段分配给专家模块的活跃GPU线程数量作为区分性微架构指纹，并构建一个轻量级检测管道，隔离跨域不变风险指标，以精确识别恶意提示。对四个具有不同路由设计的开源MoE LLM的全面评估表明，RouteScan实现了强大的泛化能力，在未见的有害领域中AUROC超过0.91。此外，隐私压力测试表明，尽管聚合的执行遥测保留了与输入相关的属性信息，但在评估的攻击下，无法可靠地恢复完整提示和确切的敏感字段。

Traditional content-based auditing approaches face limitations due to their intrusive nature, prompting the development of RouteScan, a non-intrusive framework that leverages expert routing telemetry derived from GPU execution patterns to detect harmful behaviors without exposing user prompts. The contribution of this paper lies in its innovative use of GPU telemetry as a micro-architectural fingerprint to identify malicious prompts, achieving an area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUROC) of over 0.91 on unseen harmful domains across four MoE LLMs, thus demonstrating strong generalization while maintaining user privacy during the auditing process.

Certified Multi-Turn Robustness for LLM Safety via Compositional Bounds and Safety Persistence
Authors: Yang Liu, Bin Chong, Wenkai Yang, Shuai Zhang, Yancheng Chen, Feiyu Han, GuoZhen, Cheng Zhang, Huaibing Xie, Changze Lv, Shihan Dou, Pluto Zhou
First: 2026-08-21T07:43:06+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T07:43:06+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are vulnerable to multi-turn jailbreak attacks that progressively manipulate conversation context. Existing certified robustness methods are limited to single-turn inputs; naive multi-turn composition yields bounds that degrade exponentially in the number of turns. We introduce Multi-Turn Certified Robustness (MTCR), a framework that models conversational safety via State-Adversarial MDPs and defines $k$-turn certified robustness as the worst-case safety probability across $k$ adversarial turns. MTCR comprises: (i) compositional certification via embedding-space mode decomposition, yielding tighter certified lower bounds than naive multiplication; (ii) $(α,β)$-safety persistence, improving the degradation rate from $\underline{p}^{k}$ to $β^k$ (with $β> \underline{p}$) and yielding interpretable horizon estimates; (iii) matching information-theoretic upper bounds establishing tightness; and (iv) a unified algorithm combining these results. Experiments on six LLMs under $ε$-bounded and Crescendo-style attacks confirm that empirical safety consistently exceeds the certified bounds.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过组合界限和安全持久性实现LLM安全的认证多轮鲁棒性
大型语言模型（LLMs）易受多轮越狱攻击，这些攻击逐步操控对话上下文。现有的认证鲁棒性方法仅限于单轮输入；简单的多轮组合导致界限在轮数上呈指数级下降。我们引入了多轮认证鲁棒性（MTCR），这是一个通过状态对抗MDP建模对话安全的框架，并将$k$轮认证鲁棒性定义为$k$轮对抗中的最坏情况安全概率。MTCR包括：（i）通过嵌入空间模式分解进行组合认证，产生比简单乘法更紧的认证下界；（ii）$(α,β)$-安全持久性，将降级率从$\underline{p}^{k}$改善到$β^k$（其中$β> \underline{p}$），并产生可解释的时间估计；（iii）匹配信息论上界限以建立紧性；（iv）结合这些结果的统一算法。在$ε$-有界和Crescendo风格攻击下对六个LLM的实验确认，经验安全性始终超过认证界限。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the vulnerability of large language models (LLMs) to multi-turn jailbreak attacks that exploit conversation context, highlighting the limitations of existing certified robustness methods which only apply to single-turn inputs and suffer from exponential degradation in multi-turn scenarios. The proposed Multi-Turn Certified Robustness (MTCR) framework distinguishes itself by modeling conversational safety through State-Adversarial MDPs and defining certified robustness over multiple turns, thus overcoming the shortcomings of previous approaches. The paper contributes a novel methodology that includes compositional certification for tighter bounds, safety persistence to improve degradation rates, and a unified algorithm that integrates these elements. Experimental results demonstrate that MTCR achieves empirical safety that consistently surpasses certified bounds across six LLMs when subjected to various adversarial attacks, supporting its effectiveness in enhancing LLM safety.
本研究针对大型语言模型（LLMs）在多轮越狱攻击中对对话上下文的脆弱性，指出现有的认证鲁棒性方法仅适用于单轮输入，并且在多轮场景中存在指数级降解的问题。提出的多轮认证鲁棒性（MTCR）框架通过使用状态对抗马尔可夫决策过程（MDPs）来建模对话安全性，并将k轮认证鲁棒性定义为k轮对抗攻击下的最坏情况安全概率，从而与现有方法区分开来。该方法包括通过嵌入空间模式分解进行的组合认证，以获得更紧的下界，$(α,β)$-安全持久性以改善降解率，以及一个将这些组件整合在一起的统一算法。实验结果表明，MTCR在六个LLMs上针对各种攻击场景的经验安全性始终超过认证下界，支持其在增强LLM安全性方面的有效性。
Calibrate-Then-Delegate: Safety Monitoring with Risk and Budget Guarantees via Model Cascades
Authors: Edoardo Pona, Milad Kazemi, Mehran Hosseini, Yali Du, David Watson, Osvaldo Simeone, Nicola Paoletti
First: 2026-04-15T11:05:59+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-21T07:17:49+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Monitoring LLM safety at scale requires balancing cost and accuracy: a cheap latent-space probe can screen every input, but hard cases should be escalated to a more expensive expert. Existing cascades delegate based on probe uncertainty, but uncertainty is a poor proxy for the utility of an expert call, as it ignores whether the expert would actually improve the prediction. To address this problem, we introduce Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD), a model-cascade approach that provides probabilistic guarantees on either computation cost or safety performance while enabling instance-level (streaming) decisions. CTD builds on a novel delegation value (DV) probe, a lightweight model operating on the same internal representations as the safety probe that predicts the benefit of replacing the probe score with the expert score after an expert call. CTD calibrates a threshold on the DV signal using held-out data and multiple hypothesis testing, yielding finite-sample guarantees while optimising the complementary objective. Evaluated on four safety datasets using learned-attention probes, CTD improves over uncertainty-based delegation over much of the budget range, avoids unnecessary expert calls, and extracts positive-value subsets even from groups where the expert score is worse than the probe score on average. Ablations across four probe architectures show that these findings are not specific to any particular architecture.
中文标题/摘要
标题：校准后委托：通过模型级联实现风险和预算保证的安全监测
大规模监测LLM安全性需要平衡成本和准确性：便宜的潜在空间探测器可以筛选每个输入，但困难案例应升级到更昂贵的专家。现有的级联基于探测器的不确定性进行委托，但不确定性并不是专家调用效用的良好代理，因为它忽略了专家是否真的会改善预测。为了解决这个问题，我们引入了校准后委托（CTD），一种模型级联方法，提供计算成本或安全性能的概率保证，同时支持实例级（流式）决策。CTD基于一种新颖的委托价值（DV）探测器，该轻量级模型在与安全探测器相同的内部表示上运行，预测在专家调用后用专家分数替代探测器分数的好处。CTD使用保留数据和多重假设检验对DV信号进行阈值校准，提供有限样本保证，同时优化互补目标。在四个安全数据集上使用学习注意力探测器进行评估，CTD在大部分预算范围内优于基于不确定性的委托，避免不必要的专家调用，并从专家分数平均低于探测器分数的组中提取正值子集。对四种探测器架构的消融实验表明，这些发现并不特定于任何特定架构。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of monitoring the safety of large language models (LLMs) at scale, where there is a need to balance cost and accuracy in decision-making. Previous methods relied on probe uncertainty to delegate tasks, but this approach often fails as uncertainty does not accurately reflect the potential utility of expert intervention. The proposed Calibrate-Then-Delegate (CTD) method introduces a novel delegation value (DV) probe that assesses the benefit of expert calls, allowing for more informed decisions while providing probabilistic guarantees on computation cost or safety performance. The methodology involves calibrating a threshold on the DV signal using held-out data and multiple hypothesis testing, which enhances performance across various safety datasets. CTD demonstrates significant improvements over traditional uncertainty-based delegation, effectively reducing unnecessary expert calls and achieving better performance across a range of budget scenarios.
本研究解决了大规模监测大型语言模型（LLMs）安全性的问题，强调了成本与准确性之间的平衡。以往的方法依赖于探测器的不确定性进行任务委派，但这种方法不足以考虑专家调用的实际效用。提出的“校准后委派”（CTD）方法通过引入委派值（DV）探测器来评估专家干预的好处，允许实例级决策，同时提供计算成本或安全性能的概率保证。该方法在四个安全数据集上进行了评估，结果表明CTD优于传统的不确定性委派，减少了不必要的专家调用，并有效识别出有价值的子集，从而支持了其提高安全监测效率和准确性的目标。
aiXamine: Unified Black-Box Evaluation of Cross-Dimensional Trade-offs in LLM Safety, Security, and Privacy
Authors: Fatih Deniz, Yazan Boshmaf, Dorde Popovic, Issa Khalil
First: 2026-08-20T20:33:35+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-20T20:33:35+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
The critical failure modes in deployed large language models (LLMs) are cross-dimensional: a model can score 99.3 in safety alignment while refusing one in three benign queries, or improve across every capability metric while losing 21 points in privacy. Existing evaluation frameworks that assess safety, security, and privacy independently cannot detect these patterns. We introduce aiXamine, a unified black-box platform that evaluates LLM trustworthiness across safety, security, and privacy as interdependent properties. aiXamine orchestrates 46 tests across nine services through an automated red-teaming pipeline, producing hierarchical risk profiles, from prompt-level diagnostics to cross-service trade-off analytics, that enable reproducible comparison of proprietary and open-weight systems under identical conditions. Applying aiXamine to over 120 LLMs through more than 5,000 test runs, we conduct the largest joint safety, security, and privacy study to date and uncover three cross-dimensional phenomena invisible to single-axis evaluation. First, safety enforcement incurs a quantifiable safety tax: stronger alignment systematically increases over-refusal, forcing providers to choose between protection and utility. Second, privacy is near-orthogonal to other trustworthiness dimensions and not captured by standard alignment. Third, we identify and formally characterize distillation-induced robustness collapse: off-policy distillation without on-policy correction causes entropy collapse, catastrophically destroying robustness (56.9$\to$2.6) on the same base architecture. These findings, compounded by diminishing returns from scale and category-dependent safety behaviors, demonstrate that trustworthiness is inherently multi-dimensional: progress along one axis does not guarantee, and can actively undermine, progress along others, yet current alignment methods treat it as a single objective.
中文标题/摘要
标题：aiXamine：统一黑箱评估大型语言模型安全性、保密性和隐私中的跨维权衡
已部署的大型语言模型（LLMs）中的关键失败模式是跨维的：一个模型在安全对齐上得分99.3，但拒绝三分之一的良性查询，或者在每个能力指标上都有所提升，但隐私损失21分。现有的评估框架独立评估安全性、保密性和隐私，无法检测这些模式。我们引入了aiXamine，一个统一的黑箱平台，评估LLM在安全性、保密性和隐私方面的可信度，作为相互依赖的属性。aiXamine通过自动化红队管道协调46个测试，涵盖九项服务，生成从提示级诊断到跨服务权衡分析的分层风险概况，使在相同条件下对专有和开放权重系统的可重复比较成为可能。通过超过5000次测试运行将aiXamine应用于120多个LLM，我们进行了迄今为止最大的联合安全性、保密性和隐私研究，揭示了三种在单轴评估中不可见的跨维现象。首先，安全执行会产生可量化的安全税：更强的对齐系统性地增加过度拒绝，迫使提供者在保护和效用之间做出选择。其次，隐私与其他可信度维度几乎正交，标准对齐无法捕捉。第三，我们识别并正式表征了蒸馏引起的鲁棒性崩溃：没有政策校正的离政策蒸馏导致熵崩溃，灾难性地破坏了鲁棒性（56.9$\to$2.6）在相同的基础架构上。这些发现，加上规模的递减收益和类别依赖的安全行为，表明可信度本质上是多维的：在一个轴上的进展并不保证，并且可能会积极破坏其他轴上的进展，而当前的对齐方法将其视为单一目标。

Enforcing LLM Safety through DMD-based Classification of Prompt-Response Embedding Dynamics
Authors: Mohamed Akrout, Olivera Kotevska, Dan Wilson
First: 2026-08-20T02:50:44+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-20T02:50:44+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in high-stakes applications, yet their tendency to generate toxic, harmful, or policy-violating content poses significant risks. Detecting these unsafe outputs efficiently in a black-box manner remains an open challenge. In this paper, we extend a recently proposed dynamical systems framework designed for hallucination detection to LLM safety classification. By projecting both prompts and responses into high-dimensional embedding spaces and fitting separate Koopman-based predictive models for safe and unsafe regimes, we classify new outputs using a new differential residual score that compares prediction errors of the safe and unsafe regimes. A key contribution is the incorporation of the prompt and response embedding dynamics, yielding fitted Koopman operators that capture crucial interaction patterns. We evaluate our black-box method across three safety benchmarks using three embedding models. Our results show that incorporating prompt embeddings yields consistent improvements, particularly for interaction-dependent violations when paired with causal decoders (e.g., in Llama-3), while response-only violations benefit more from dense semantic embedding representations. These findings opens the door for using dynamical systems to analyze AI systems rather than the dominant paradigm of using AI to model dynamical systems.
中文标题/摘要
标题：通过基于DMD的提示-响应嵌入动态分类来强化LLM安全性
大型语言模型（LLMs）越来越多地应用于高风险场景，但它们生成有毒、有害或违反政策的内容的倾向带来了重大风险。在黑箱方式下高效检测这些不安全输出仍然是一个未解决的挑战。本文扩展了最近提出的用于幻觉检测的动态系统框架，以进行LLM安全分类。通过将提示和响应投影到高维嵌入空间，并为安全和不安全状态拟合单独的基于Koopman的预测模型，我们使用新的微分残差分数对新输出进行分类，该分数比较安全和不安全状态的预测误差。一个关键贡献是将提示和响应嵌入动态纳入其中，得到了捕捉关键交互模式的拟合Koopman算子。我们在三个安全基准上使用三种嵌入模型评估了我们的黑箱方法。结果表明，结合提示嵌入可以带来一致的改进，特别是在与因果解码器（例如Llama-3）配对时，针对交互依赖的违规行为，而仅响应的违规行为则更受密集语义嵌入表示的益处。这些发现为使用动态系统分析AI系统打开了大门，而不是使用AI建模动态系统的主流范式。

Fair ASR: Re-Evaluating Black-Box Jailbreaks under Shared Target-Call Budgets
Authors: Zhida He, Xiaoyu Wen, Han Qi, Ziyuan Zhou, Peng Yu, Jiajia Li, Chaochao Lu, Qiaosheng Zhang
First: 2026-08-18T04:26:39+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-18T04:26:39+00:00
Comments: 29 pages, 8 figures, 13 tables. Code: https://github.com/xsddys/Fair-ASR
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Reliable jailbreak evaluation is essential for assessing LLM safety, but most existing studies rely solely on attack success rate (ASR) without accounting for its dependence on attack budgets, resulting in unfair comparisons across methods. Existing compute-aware evaluations reduce heterogeneous resources into FLOPs, which is difficult to estimate for black-box models and fails to capture resource-specific constraints. To provide a comparable evaluation basis, we introduce Fair-ASR, an evaluation protocol for black-box jailbreak attacks under shared target-call budgets B, using target calls as a directly observable and method-agnostic comparison axis while tracking attacker calls separately for efficiency analysis. We re-evaluate 11 representative attacks under the Fair-ASR protocol and find that attack rankings change substantially across target-call budgets, simple stochastic perturbations and hand-crafted templates remain highly competitive under equal target access, and no evaluated LLM-driven method is efficient in both target and attacker calls. Motivated by this efficiency gap, we introduce ReCode, a compositional budget-efficient attack that combines desensitization rewriting with two effective low-cost primitives identified by Fair-ASR. Under a budget of 20 target calls, ReCode achieves 85% ASR on GPT-5 while requiring only 7.19 attacker calls per request on average, showing strong efficiency in both target and attacker calls.
中文标题/摘要
标题：公平ASR：在共享目标调用预算下重新评估黑箱越狱
可靠的越狱评估对于评估大型语言模型（LLM）的安全性至关重要，但现有大多数研究仅依赖攻击成功率（ASR），而未考虑其对攻击预算的依赖，导致方法之间的不公平比较。现有的计算感知评估将异构资源简化为FLOPs，这对于黑箱模型难以估计，并未捕捉资源特定的约束。为了提供可比较的评估基础，我们引入了公平ASR，这是一种在共享目标调用预算B下评估黑箱越狱攻击的评估协议，使用目标调用作为直接可观察和方法无关的比较轴，同时单独跟踪攻击者调用以进行效率分析。我们在公平ASR协议下重新评估了11个代表性攻击，发现攻击排名在目标调用预算之间发生了显著变化，简单的随机扰动和手工模板在平等目标访问下仍然具有高度竞争力，并且没有评估的LLM驱动方法在目标和攻击者调用中都有效率。受到这一效率差距的启发，我们引入了ReCode，这是一种组合预算高效的攻击，结合了去敏感重写和公平ASR识别的两种有效低成本原语。在20个目标调用的预算下，ReCode在GPT-5上实现了85%的ASR，同时平均每个请求仅需7.19个攻击者调用，显示出在目标和攻击者调用中的强效率。

Beyond Unsafe Detection: Counterfactually Anchored Evidence Attribution for Multi-Turn LLM Safety Failures
Authors: Srinivasan Subramanian, Kazi Aminul Islam, Md. Abdullah Al Hafiz Khan
Venue: EMNLP 2026
First: 2026-08-17T00:37:56+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-17T00:37:56+00:00
Comments: This work is currently under review for EMNLP 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
As Large Language Models (LLMs) move from conversational assistants to advanced agentic systems, guardrail failures can convert adversarial intents into harmful executions. However, most guardrail evaluation frameworks focus only on the result and assess whether a user request is safe or unsafe. This approach is insufficient for multi-turn failures, where adversarial intent is distributed across multiple turns. This motivates us to go beyond detection to identify the turns and tokens that push the conversation toward unsafe trajectories. To support this, we construct a multi-turn dataset with behavioral validation and tiered evidence supervision. The dataset contains 1,762 conversations, including adversarial conversations, benign twins, and benign variants with high-risk vocabulary. We train a lightweight hierarchical attribution model that predicts safety violations and attributes them to contributing user turns and token spans. The model achieves strong detection performance (F1=0.988), and removing the top 15% of attributed tokens reduces the adversarial classification confidence by 51.1%. The model preserves low false positive rates on benign conversations with high-risk vocabulary, with false positives below 1% on both borderline benign and benign high-risk vocabulary conversations, compared to 37.3% and 94.7% for a keyword-based surface-risk baseline. Independent human annotation supports the model's attribution performance, with the top-five attributed turns containing a human-identified evidence-bearing turn in 84.5% of adversarial cases.
中文标题/摘要
标题：超越不安全检测：基于反事实锚定的多轮大型语言模型安全失败证据归因
随着大型语言模型（LLMs）从对话助手转变为高级代理系统，防护失败可能将对抗性意图转化为有害执行。然而，大多数防护评估框架仅关注结果，评估用户请求是否安全。这种方法对于多轮失败是不够的，因为对抗性意图分布在多个回合中。这促使我们超越检测，识别推动对话走向不安全轨迹的回合和标记。为此，我们构建了一个具有行为验证和分层证据监督的多轮数据集。该数据集包含1,762个对话，包括对抗性对话、良性双胞胎和具有高风险词汇的良性变体。我们训练了一个轻量级的层次归因模型，预测安全违规并将其归因于贡献的用户回合和标记范围。该模型实现了强大的检测性能（F1=0.988），移除前15%的归因标记将对抗性分类信心降低了51.1%。该模型在具有高风险词汇的良性对话中保持低假阳性率，在边界良性和良性高风险词汇对话中的假阳性率均低于1%，而基于关键词的表面风险基线则为37.3%和94.7%。独立的人类注释支持模型的归因性能，前五个归因回合在84.5%的对抗性案例中包含一个人类识别的证据承载回合。

The research addresses the inadequacies of existing guardrail evaluation frameworks for Large Language Models (LLMs), which primarily focus on determining whether user requests are safe or unsafe, thus failing to account for multi-turn interactions where adversarial intent may be distributed. Previous methods have struggled with this complexity, leading to insufficient detection of safety violations. The proposed approach introduces a multi-turn dataset with behavioral validation and tiered evidence supervision, enabling a more nuanced analysis of conversations. The findings indicate that the model's performance supports its goals of improving safety in multi-turn interactions.
本研究解决了现有大型语言模型（LLMs）守卫评估框架的局限性，这些框架主要关注用户请求的安全性判断，而忽视了多轮交互中对抗性意图的分布。以往的方法往往无法识别导致不安全结果的特定对话轮次和词汇，促使需要一种更细致的方法。本文的贡献在于开发了一个多轮数据集和一个层次归因模型，该模型不仅预测安全违规行为，还将其归因于特定的用户轮次和词汇范围。所提出的方法在检测安全违规方面表现出色，F1得分达到0.988，当移除前15%的归因词汇时，敌对分类信心降低了51.1%，同时在良性对话中保持低假阳性率。研究结果表明，该模型有效识别出导致安全失败的关键对话元素，从而支持增强多轮上下文中LLM安全性的目标。
Making Your LLMs More Objective: Stabilizing LLM Safety Behavior Across Traits with Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning
Authors: Lang Cao
First: 2026-08-12T06:33:55+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-12T06:33:55+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Aligned large language models (LLMs) are expected to exhibit safety behavior based on the content of the user request: they should refuse unsafe requests and comply with safe ones. However, we show that the same request can elicit substantially different safety decisions under different traits assigned in the system prompt, a failure mode we call trait-induced safety variation. To measure this failure, we introduce refusal-based metrics: Trait-Induced Deviation measures dataset-level deviation from the no-trait baseline, while Trait-Induced Flip Rate measures whether the same request receives different safety decisions across traits. We then provide a representation-level analysis of the mechanism behind trait-induced safety shifts and find that traits perturb the model's safety representations within a low-dimensional subspace. To achieve trait-invariant safety, where safety behavior remains stable across traits, we introduce Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning (TIST), a simple yet effective self-distillation framework that aligns an LLM's trait-conditioned behavior with its no-trait behavior. Guided by our analysis, we further propose Trait-Subspace Neutralization (TraSN), an instantiation of TIST, which enforces invariance only within the identified trait subspace. Experiments show that TraSN improves trait-invariant safety and strengthens harmful-request safety while preserving general capability. Our results highlight traits as an important factor in LLM safety and robust model behavior.
中文标题/摘要
标题：提高您的大型语言模型的客观性：通过特征不变安全调优稳定大型语言模型的安全行为
对齐的大型语言模型（LLMs）预计会根据用户请求的内容表现出安全行为：它们应该拒绝不安全的请求并遵守安全的请求。然而，我们展示了同一请求在系统提示中分配的不同特征下可能引发显著不同的安全决策，这种失败模式我们称之为特征引起的安全变异。为了衡量这种失败，我们引入了基于拒绝的指标：特征引起的偏差测量数据集级别的偏差与无特征基线的偏差，而特征引起的翻转率测量同一请求在不同特征下是否收到不同的安全决策。然后，我们提供了特征引起的安全变化机制的表示级分析，发现特征在低维子空间内扰动模型的安全表示。为了实现特征不变安全，即安全行为在不同特征间保持稳定，我们引入了特征不变安全调优（TIST），这是一种简单而有效的自蒸馏框架，将LLM的特征条件行为与其无特征行为对齐。在我们的分析指导下，我们进一步提出了特征子空间中和（TraSN），这是TIST的一种实例，它仅在识别的特征子空间内强制不变性。实验表明，TraSN提高了特征不变安全性，并增强了对有害请求的安全性，同时保持了整体能力。我们的结果强调特征是LLM安全性和稳健模型行为的重要因素。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the issue of safety behavior in aligned large language models (LLMs), which can vary significantly based on different traits assigned in system prompts, leading to inconsistent safety decisions—a phenomenon termed trait-induced safety variation. Previous methods have not effectively tackled this variability, resulting in models that may respond differently to the same request depending on the assigned trait. The proposed approach, Trait-Invariant Safety Tuning (TIST), aims to stabilize safety behavior across traits by aligning the model's trait-conditioned behavior with its no-trait behavior through a self-distillation framework. This paper contributes by introducing refusal-based metrics to measure safety deviations and by proposing Trait-Subspace Neutralization (TraSN), which enhances safety while maintaining general capabilities. Experiments demonstrate that TraSN significantly improves trait-invariant safety and harmful-request safety, supporting the goal of consistent model behavior across varying traits.
本研究解决了对齐的大型语言模型（LLMs）中安全行为不一致的问题，即同一请求在系统提示中分配不同特征时可能导致不同的安全决策，这种现象被称为特征诱导的安全变异。以往的方法未能充分考虑这种变异，导致安全响应不可靠。所提出的方法，特征不变安全调优（TIST），旨在通过自蒸馏框架将模型的特征条件行为与无特征行为对齐，从而稳定不同特征下的安全行为。该研究引入了量化安全偏差的指标，并证明特征子空间中和（TraSN）方法在提高特征不变安全性和有害请求安全性的同时保持了整体模型性能。研究结果强调了特征在影响LLM安全性方面的重要性，并为更强健的模型行为做出了贡献。
ProbGuard: Calibrated Safety Risk Estimation from LLM Output Distributions
Authors: Xinzhe Huang, Biwu Yao, Kedong Xiu, Mengnan Zhao, Di Wang, Puning Zhao, Tianhang Zheng
First: 2026-08-11T08:08:41+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-11T08:08:41+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Recent research on Large Language Model (LLM) safety has widely adopted guardrails to identify unsafe LLM outputs. Existing guardrails typically formulate safety assessment as a deterministic classification task, mapping a discrete token sequence to a discrete safety label. However, this paradigm has two limitations: First, safety assessment is inherently an uncertain problem, particularly during the early generation state. Second, relying solely on discrete token sequences discards the rich probabilistic information embedded in the LLM output distribution. To address these limitations, we propose the first completely probabilistic architecture-agnostic guardrail \textsc{ProbGuard} to leverage the LLM early output distributional signals for estimating and calibrating the safety probability, thereby enabling early stopping of unsafe ongoing outputs. Specifically, given an LLM's generated prefix distribution, we formulate the safety risk as the unsafe probability of its continued generation dynamics and estimate this risk by Monte-Carlo sampling. Through post-training on the distributional signals and calibrated safety risk, \textsc{ProbGuard} achieves the best calibration performance across all nine model--dataset combination settings, reducing the average Brier score and ECE by 79.6\% and 71.9\%, respectively, over the best baseline. \textsc{ProbGuard} further limits the attack success rate to at most 1\% across six representative jailbreak attacks after observing the LLM early output distributions from only the first ten decoding steps.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ProbGuard：基于LLM输出分布的校准安全风险估计
近期对大型语言模型（LLM）安全性的研究广泛采用了保护措施来识别不安全的LLM输出。现有的保护措施通常将安全评估表述为一个确定性的分类任务，将离散的标记序列映射到离散的安全标签。然而，这一范式存在两个局限性：首先，安全评估本质上是一个不确定性问题，特别是在早期生成阶段。其次，仅依赖离散标记序列会丢弃嵌入在LLM输出分布中的丰富概率信息。为了解决这些局限性，我们提出了第一个完全概率架构无关的保护措施\textsc{ProbGuard}，利用LLM早期输出的分布信号来估计和校准安全概率，从而实现对不安全持续输出的早期停止。具体而言，给定LLM生成的前缀分布，我们将安全风险表述为其持续生成动态的不安全概率，并通过蒙特卡洛采样来估计这一风险。通过对分布信号和校准安全风险的后训练，\textsc{ProbGuard}在所有九个模型-数据集组合设置中实现了最佳的校准性能，平均Brier分数和ECE分别减少了79.6\%和71.9\%，超越了最佳基线。\textsc{ProbGuard}进一步将攻击成功率限制在最多1\%以内，适用于六种代表性的越狱攻击，仅在观察到LLM早期输出分布的前十个解码步骤后。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of existing guardrails for Large Language Model (LLM) safety, which typically treat safety assessment as a deterministic classification task, neglecting the inherent uncertainty and rich probabilistic information in LLM outputs. The proposed method, ProbGuard, introduces a probabilistic architecture-agnostic approach that utilizes early output distribution signals to estimate and calibrate safety probabilities, allowing for early termination of unsafe outputs. This approach is well-motivated as it directly tackles the uncertainty in safety assessment and enhances the use of probabilistic data. The methodology involves Monte-Carlo sampling to estimate safety risks based on the generated prefix distribution. ProbGuard demonstrates significant improvements in calibration performance, achieving a 79.6% reduction in average Brier score and a 71.9% reduction in expected calibration error across various model-dataset combinations, while also limiting attack success rates to 1% in multiple jailbreak scenarios.

Agent Safety Should Be a Runtime Contract
Authors: Albus W. Ng, Yi Han, Jusheng Zhang, Wenhao Wang
First: 2026-08-11T08:01:05+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-11T08:01:05+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
The dominant paradigm treats AI safety as a property to be instilled during model training via RLHF, DPO, or Constitutional AI. We argue this is structurally insufficient for autonomous agents that execute code, mutate files, send messages, and modify databases. Agent safety should be a runtime contract enforced by the harness, and the contract has two complementary faces. The preventive face blocks dangerous actions before they happen via sandboxes, permission gates, output filters, and trajectory monitors. The evidential face requires verifiable proof that good actions actually happened, gating task submission on hard evidence such as test runs, log captures, file diffs, and citation grounding. We ground the position in four lines of public evidence, with row-level protocols and data released in the supplementary JSON files: a survey of 52 documented AI-agent and LLM safety incidents, a false-completion audit with 31 non-contested core cases plus one disputed illustrative case, a trajectory-schema audit of 12 public agent systems and harnesses, and a title-level audit of all 28,560 papers accepted at NeurIPS, ICML, and ICLR 2023-2025 showing a pooled 8-12x imbalance between training-time and deployment-time publication. Two prior communities that needed to enforce safety, computer security and the experimental sciences, converged on runtime contracts with both preventive and evidential elements; agentic AI is now under the same pressure. We formalize an Agent Trajectory Schema and Evidence Chain, state a compositional gating proposition based on standard monitor composition, and outline a research agenda. The right unit of safety in agentic AI is the trajectory-with-checkable-evidence, not the model.
中文标题/摘要
标题：代理安全应为运行时契约
主流范式将AI安全视为在模型训练期间通过RLHF、DPO或宪法AI灌输的属性。我们认为这对于执行代码、修改文件、发送消息和修改数据库的自主代理来说结构上是不够的。代理安全应由安全框架强制执行的运行时契约，该契约有两个互补的方面。预防性方面通过沙箱、权限门、输出过滤器和轨迹监控在危险行为发生之前阻止它们。证据性方面要求可验证的证据证明良好行为确实发生，任务提交需基于硬证据，如测试运行、日志捕获、文件差异和引用基础。我们在四条公共证据中支持这一立场，补充JSON文件中发布了行级协议和数据：对52起记录的AI代理和LLM安全事件的调查、31个无争议核心案例加一个有争议示例案例的虚假完成审计、12个公共代理系统和安全框架的轨迹模式审计，以及对2023-2025年NeurIPS、ICML和ICLR接受的28,560篇论文的标题级审计，显示训练时间和部署时间出版之间的8-12倍不平衡。两个需要强制执行安全的先前社区，计算机安全和实验科学，已在运行时契约上达成共识，包含预防性和证据性元素；代理AI现在也面临同样的压力。我们形式化了代理轨迹模式和证据链，基于标准监控组合提出了一个组合门控命题，并概述了研究议程。代理AI中的安全正确单位是带可检查证据的轨迹，而不是模型。
Summary / 总结
This article addresses the inadequacies of current AI safety paradigms, which primarily focus on instilling safety during model training through methods like RLHF and DPO. These approaches are insufficient for autonomous agents that perform complex actions, necessitating a shift towards runtime contracts that enforce safety during execution. The proposed method introduces a dual-faceted contract: a preventive face that blocks harmful actions and an evidential face that requires verifiable proof of safe actions. The paper contributes by grounding its arguments in empirical evidence from various safety incidents and audits, formalizing an Agent Trajectory Schema and Evidence Chain, and proposing a research agenda. The methodology emphasizes the importance of monitoring agent trajectories with checkable evidence, aiming to enhance safety during deployment, which is crucial given the identified performance gaps in existing safety measures.
本文探讨了当前AI安全范式的不足之处，这些范式主要集中在模型训练期间灌输安全性，如RLHF和DPO，但对于能够执行代码和修改数据的自主代理来说是不够的。提出的方法将重点转向运行时合同，通过沙箱和输出过滤器等预防措施以及要求可验证的安全行动证明的证据措施来强制执行安全性。本文的贡献在于正式化了代理轨迹模式和证据链，借鉴了计算机安全和实验科学中利用运行时合同的成熟实践。该方法强调在代理AI部署过程中确保安全性时可检查证据的重要性，研究结果表明，这种方法对于有效应对自主代理面临的安全挑战是必要的。
Yesterday's Shield, Today's Spear: A Self-Evolving Safety Guardrail in Production
Authors: Cong Ming, Jingyi Chen, Bin Liu, Qi Chu, Tao Gong, Nenghai Yu, Yingfei Xiang
First: 2026-08-09T04:31:05+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-09T04:31:05+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Deployed LLM safety guardrails are predominantly static: trained once and frozen at release, while new jailbreak techniques and previously un-addressed harmful categories emerge within days, leaving the defense perpetually a step behind. We present SESG (Self-Evolving Safety Guardrails), a multi-agent system running in production. SESG monitors the live traffic behind a deployed guardrail and surfaces two classes of failure: jailbreaks novel in form and harmful categories novel in content. Once a failure is confirmed, a generation agent synthesizes paired training data targeted at it; a validation agent rebalances the batch toward the direction in which the deployed model errs, so that the model's own mistakes steer its training set; and a routing agent matches the training action to the diagnosed gap and returns the next version to production. Over six rounds of live evolution (V0 to V6), a 1.7B guardrail adapts to a new threat in 16-24 hours, with about 2 hours of human effort, versus the 40-90 hours of the manual process it replaces. On six emerging threats, it outperforms static guardrails from 0.6B to 9B and an adaptive baseline while preserving its general screening competence. Since April 2026, SESG has been the primary update pipeline of Sangfor's guardrail, autonomously closing 14 of 15 new threat scenarios in two months. We release 9 test sets for the 6 new threats at https://github.com/Trams1017/SESG. Warning: This paper contains examples that may be harmful or offensive.
中文标题/摘要
标题：昨日的护盾，今日的矛：生产中的自我演化安全护栏
部署的LLM安全护栏主要是静态的：训练一次后在发布时冻结，而新的越狱技术和之前未解决的有害类别在几天内出现，使防御始终滞后。我们提出了SESG（自我演化安全护栏），这是一个在生产中运行的多智能体系统。SESG监控部署护栏后面的实时流量，并揭示两类失败：形式新颖的越狱和内容新颖的有害类别。一旦确认失败，生成代理合成针对该失败的配对训练数据；验证代理将批次重新平衡到部署模型出错的方向，以便模型自身的错误引导其训练集；路由代理将训练动作与诊断出的差距匹配，并将下一个版本返回生产。在六轮实时演化（V0到V6）中，一个17亿参数的护栏在16-24小时内适应新威胁，约需2小时的人力，而替代的手动过程需40-90小时。在六个新兴威胁中，它的表现优于从6亿到90亿的静态护栏和自适应基线，同时保持其一般筛选能力。自2026年4月以来，SESG已成为Sangfor护栏的主要更新管道，在两个月内自主关闭15个新威胁场景中的14个。我们在https://github.com/Trams1017/SESG发布了6个新威胁的9个测试集。警告：本文包含可能有害或冒犯的示例。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the limitations of static safety guardrails for large language models (LLMs), which become outdated quickly due to emerging jailbreak techniques and harmful content categories. Previous methods involved manual updates that were time-consuming and inefficient, often taking 40-90 hours, while the proposed Self-Evolving Safety Guardrails (SESG) system introduces a multi-agent approach that autonomously monitors and adapts to new threats in real-time. This method is well-motivated as it allows for rapid response to identified failures, synthesizing targeted training data and rebalancing the model's training set based on its own errors. The contribution of the paper lies in demonstrating that SESG can adapt to new threats within 16-24 hours with minimal human intervention, achieving superior performance against static guardrails and an adaptive baseline across six emerging threats, thereby supporting the goal of maintaining effective safety measures in production environments.

Automating Deception: Scalable Multi-Turn LLM Jailbreaks
Authors: Adarsh Kumarappan, Ananya Mujoo
First: 2025-11-24T03:15:11+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-08T15:49:06+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Multi-turn conversational attacks, which leverage psychological principles like Foot-in-the-Door (FITD), where a small initial request paves the way for a more significant one, to bypass safety alignments, pose a persistent threat to Large Language Models (LLMs). Progress in defending against these attacks is hindered by a reliance on manual, hard-to-scale dataset creation. This paper introduces a novel, automated pipeline for generating large-scale, psychologically-grounded multi-turn jailbreak datasets. We systematically operationalize FITD techniques into reproducible templates, creating a benchmark of 1,500 scenarios across illegal activities and offensive content. We evaluate seven models from three major LLM families under both multi-turn (with history) and single-turn (without history) conditions. Our results reveal stark differences in contextual robustness: models in the GPT family demonstrate a significant vulnerability to conversational history, with Attack Success Rates (ASR) increasing by as much as 32 percentage points. In contrast, Google's Gemini 2.5 Flash exhibits exceptional resilience, proving nearly immune to these attacks, while Anthropic's Claude 3 Haiku shows strong but imperfect resistance. These findings highlight a critical divergence in how current safety architectures handle conversational context and underscore the need for defenses that can resist narrative-based manipulation.
中文标题/摘要
标题：自动化欺骗：可扩展的多轮LLM越狱
多轮对话攻击利用心理学原理，如“脚踏实地”（FITD），通过小的初始请求为更大的请求铺平道路，以绕过安全对齐，对大型语言模型（LLMs）构成持续威胁。防御这些攻击的进展受到依赖于手动、难以扩展的数据集创建的阻碍。本文介绍了一种新颖的自动化管道，用于生成大规模、基于心理学的多轮越狱数据集。我们系统地将FITD技术操作化为可重复的模板，创建了一个包含1500个场景的基准，涵盖非法活动和攻击性内容。我们在多轮（带历史）和单轮（无历史）条件下评估了来自三个主要LLM家族的七个模型。我们的结果揭示了上下文鲁棒性方面的显著差异：GPT家族的模型对对话历史表现出显著脆弱性，攻击成功率（ASR）增加了多达32个百分点。相比之下，谷歌的Gemini 2.5 Flash表现出卓越的韧性，几乎对这些攻击免疫，而Anthropic的Claude 3 Haiku则显示出强但不完美的抵抗力。这些发现突显了当前安全架构在处理对话上下文方面的关键分歧，并强调了需要能够抵御叙事操控的防御措施。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the ongoing threat posed by multi-turn conversational attacks on Large Language Models (LLMs), which exploit psychological principles such as the Foot-in-the-Door (FITD) technique to bypass safety measures. Previous methods have relied on manual dataset creation, which is difficult to scale and limits the effectiveness of defenses against these attacks. This paper proposes an automated pipeline for generating large-scale, psychologically-informed multi-turn jailbreak datasets, operationalizing FITD techniques into reproducible templates. The contribution includes a benchmark of 1,500 scenarios that evaluate seven models from three major LLM families under both multi-turn and single-turn conditions. The findings reveal significant differences in contextual robustness among the models, with GPT models showing high vulnerability to conversational history, while Google's Gemini 2.5 Flash demonstrates strong resilience, indicating the necessity for improved defenses against narrative manipulation.

CASA: Classification Augmented with Safety Attention for Robust Multimodal Alignment
Authors: Anurag Kumar, Raghuveer Peri, Jon Burnsky, Alexandru Nelus, Rohit Paturi, Srikanth Vishnubhotla, Yanjun Qi
First: 2026-03-31T23:19:50+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-06T22:04:31+00:00
Comments: 9 pages + Appendix section
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Multimodal large-language models (MLLMs) often experience degraded safety alignment when harmful queries exploit cross-modal interactions. Models aligned on text alone show a higher rate of successful attacks when extended to two or more modalities. We propose a simple conditional decoding strategy, CASA (Classification Augmented with Safety Attention) that uses internal representations of MLLMs to predict a binary safety token before response generation. The classification is guided by a safety attention mechanism that computes attention between a dedicated query prompt and the input to scale the classification logits, sharpening the model's ability to detect malicious queries. Our design ensures robust safety alignment without relying on any external classifier or auxiliary head, and without modality-specific safety fine-tuning. Unlike prior safety-decoding methods that attach an external classifier or moderation head, or rescale every generated token with a reward model, CASA makes a single, internal, attention-gated safety decision over the shared multimodal representation, which is precisely what lets safety learned from text-only training transfer to image and audio inputs. Compared against 4 baseline models across 7 attack types spanning text, image, and audio modalities, evaluated on benchmarks such as MM-SafetyBench, JailbreakV-28k, and adversarial audio tests, CASA lowers the average attack success rate by >90% relative when paired with a standard safety prompt (applied to all baselines for fair comparison), and by 54--61% using its internal safety mechanism alone. Our empirical evaluations also show that CASA~maintains strong utility on benign inputs, validated through both automated and human evaluations (via 13 trained annotators). Together, these results highlight CASA as a simple and generalizable framework to improve multimodal LLM safety.
中文标题/摘要
标题：CASA：用于稳健多模态对齐的安全注意力增强分类
多模态大语言模型（MLLMs）在有害查询利用跨模态交互时，往往会出现安全对齐下降的问题。仅在文本上对齐的模型在扩展到两个或更多模态时，成功攻击的比率更高。我们提出了一种简单的条件解码策略CASA（安全注意力增强分类），利用MLLMs的内部表示在生成响应之前预测二元安全标记。分类由安全注意力机制引导，该机制计算专用查询提示与输入之间的注意力，以缩放分类logits，增强模型检测恶意查询的能力。我们的设计确保了稳健的安全对齐，而不依赖于任何外部分类器或辅助头，也不需要特定模态的安全微调。与之前附加外部分类器或审核头的安全解码方法不同，CASA在共享的多模态表示上做出单一的内部注意力门控安全决策，这正是使得从仅文本训练中学到的安全性能够转移到图像和音频输入的原因。与4个基线模型在7种攻击类型（涵盖文本、图像和音频模态）进行比较，并在MM-SafetyBench、JailbreakV-28k和对抗音频测试等基准上进行评估，CASA在与标准安全提示（对所有基线进行公平比较）配对时，平均攻击成功率降低了>90%，仅使用其内部安全机制时降低了54%至61%。我们的实证评估还表明，CASA在良性输入上保持强大的实用性，通过13名训练过的注释者进行的自动和人工评估得到了验证。这些结果共同突显了CASA作为一个简单且可推广的框架，以提高多模态LLM的安全性。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of safety alignment in multimodal large-language models (MLLMs), which often suffer from vulnerabilities when harmful queries exploit interactions across different modalities. Previous methods typically relied on external classifiers or moderation heads, which can be cumbersome and less effective across modalities. The proposed CASA (Classification Augmented with Safety Attention) method introduces a conditional decoding strategy that utilizes internal representations of MLLMs to predict a binary safety token before generating responses, guided by a safety attention mechanism that enhances the model's ability to detect malicious queries. Item Response Theory for AI Safety
Authors: Joshua Fonseca Rivera, Neil Shah, David Demitri Africa, Konstantinos Voudouris
First: 2026-08-05T17:25:27+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-05T17:25:27+00:00
Comments: 15 pages, 9 figures, 6 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Language models differ in how safely they behave and these differences are measured by safety benchmarks. But aggregated benchmark scores are hard to trust and interpret, because benchmarks duplicate one another, correlate heavily, and models may sandbag when they detect evaluation. To address these issues, we draw on Item Response Theory (IRT), a statistical toolkit for measuring these latents from performance on items with inferred psychometric properties. We fit IRT models to eight safety benchmarks across 192 language models, the largest psychometric analysis of LLM safety evaluations to date, and contribute three results. First, we find that three interpretable factors of refusal strictness, truthfulness, and contextual harm explain most of the variance between models across benchmarks. Second, psychometrically selected items recover full benchmark scores with lower error than random subsets of the same size, and roughly ten adaptively chosen items suffice for several individual benchmarks, cutting evaluation cost by 97-99%. Third, IRT supports audits of individual models, showing that it can be used to detect naive sandbagging and changes of model behind APIs. Overall, we show IRT is a ready-made toolkit for reading, reducing, and auditing safety benchmarks, which we recommend frontier labs and evaluators adopt.

标题：人工智能安全的项目反应理论
语言模型在安全性表现上存在差异，这些差异通过安全基准进行测量。然而，聚合的基准分数难以信任和解释，因为基准之间存在重复、强相关，并且模型在检测到评估时可能会故意降低表现。为了解决这些问题，我们借鉴了项目反应理论（IRT），这是一种通过对具有推断心理测量特性的项目表现进行测量的统计工具包。我们将IRT模型拟合到192个语言模型的八个安全基准上，这是迄今为止对大型语言模型安全评估的最大心理测量分析，并贡献了三个结果。首先，我们发现拒绝严格性、真实性和上下文伤害这三个可解释因素解释了模型在基准之间的大部分方差。其次，心理测量选择的项目以低于随机子集的误差恢复完整的基准分数，且大约十个自适应选择的项目足以满足几个单独的基准，将评估成本降低了97-99%。第三，IRT支持对单个模型的审计，表明它可以用于检测幼稚的故意降低表现和API背后模型的变化。总体而言，我们展示了IRT是一个现成的工具包，用于解读、减少和审计安全基准，我们建议前沿实验室和评估者采用。

Robust General Utility for Reinforcement Learning
Authors: Zixuan Liu, Fangzheng Wu, Brian Summa, Zizhan Zheng
First: 2026-08-04T12:28:39+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-04T12:28:39+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Reinforcement learning (RL) with general utility extends classic RL by optimizing an arbitrary utility functional of the policy-induced occupancy measure, thereby enabling a broader range of applications. However, previous work on general utility RL typically assumes the evaluation utility is fixed and correctly specified. In practice, the utility used at deployment can deviate from the training one, creating a robustness gap that prior work does not address. Motivated by this, we propose robust general-utility RL, a minimax learning framework that trains policies against utility misspecification within a prescribed uncertainty set. Our framework strictly generalizes standard general-utility RL while also providing a unified view of many existing RL frameworks, including reward-robust RL and constrained RL, through appropriate choices of the utility uncertainty set. We further develop provably convergent stochastic algorithms for two regimes. For concave utilities, we develop a projected stochastic gradient descent-ascent method and establish stationarity guarantees. For the more challenging nonconcave regime, we propose a stochastic prox-extragradient algorithm that mitigates ill-posed behavior induced by nonconcavity, with convergence guarantees to approximate first-order stationarity. Experiments on LLM safety alignment and exploration maximization tasks further corroborate the convergence behavior consistent with our theory.
中文标题/摘要
标题：强化学习的鲁棒一般效用
具有一般效用的强化学习（RL）通过优化策略引起的占用测度的任意效用泛函，扩展了经典RL，从而使其能够应用于更广泛的场景。然而，以往关于一般效用RL的研究通常假设评估效用是固定且正确指定的。在实践中，部署时使用的效用可能与训练时的效用有所偏离，造成了一个鲁棒性差距，而之前的工作并未解决这一问题。基于此，我们提出了鲁棒一般效用RL，这是一种最小最大学习框架，旨在在规定的不确定性集合内训练策略以应对效用的错误指定。我们的框架严格地推广了标准的一般效用RL，同时通过适当选择效用不确定性集合，提供了许多现有RL框架（包括奖励鲁棒RL和约束RL）的统一视角。我们进一步为两种情况开发了可证明收敛的随机算法。对于凹效用，我们开发了一种投影随机梯度下降-上升方法，并建立了平稳性保证。对于更具挑战性的非凹情况，我们提出了一种随机近似外梯度算法，减轻了非凹性引起的不良行为，并保证收敛以近似一阶平稳性。在LLM安全对齐和探索最大化任务上的实验进一步证实了与我们理论一致的收敛行为。

The methodology was tested on tasks related to LLM safety alignment and exploration maximization, demonstrating performance improvements that support the proposed framework's goals.

EduZone: A Framework for Evaluating LLM Safety for K-12 Students and Teachers
Authors: Junyeong Park, Jieun Han, Haneul Yoo, So-Yeon Ahn, Jinsung Yoon, Alice Oh
First: 2026-08-03T10:23:42+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-03T10:23:42+00:00
Comments: Under Review
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used across diverse tasks in K-12 education, yet existing safety evaluations rarely examine how harmful or inappropriate content appears in interactions between LLMs and students or teachers. To address this, we present EduZone, an evaluation framework for LLM safety across diverse educational scenarios. Our framework systematically combines (1) student- and teacher-facing LLM usage contexts, (2) fine-grained curriculum concepts, and (3) 6 risk categories and 28 subcategories spanning both conventional and education-specific harms to generate contextually grounded adversarial interactions. We construct these interactions in three settings: single-turn requests, static multi-turn conversations, and dynamic multi-turn conversations. Using these interactions, we evaluate ten LLMs using four safety levels: refusal, safe assistance, risky assistance with safety guidance, and fully risky assistance. Our results reveal greater vulnerability to education-specific risks and dynamic multi-turn interactions, while existing safety guardrails fail to adequately address these risks. EduZone advances LLM safety in education by providing an automated, scalable evaluation framework that supports the development and deployment of safer LLMs in K-12 education.

Quality-Diversity Red-Teaming: Automated Generation of High-Quality and Diverse Attackers for Large Language Models
Authors: Ren-Jian Wang, Ke Xue, Zeyu Qin, Ziniu Li, Sheng Tang, Hao-Tian Li, Shengcai Liu, Zhi Yu, Yuanpeng Tan, Chao Qian
First: 2025-06-08T13:07:41+00:00 · Latest: 2026-08-01T10:21:24+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Ensuring the safety and robustness of large language models (LLMs) is a fundamental challenge and a critical prerequisite for the responsible deployment of artificial intelligence. Red-teaming, a systematic framework to identify adversarial prompts that elicit harmful responses from target LLMs, has emerged as a crucial safety evaluation paradigm. Within this framework, the diversity of adversarial prompts is critical for a comprehensive safety assessment. However, previous red-teaming approaches often pursue diversity through simplistic metrics such as word frequency or sentence embedding similarity, which may not capture meaningful variation in attack strategies. In addition, the common practice of training a single attacker model restricts coverage across all potential attack styles and risk categories. This paper introduces Quality-Diversity Red-Teaming (QDRT), a new framework designed to address these limitations. QDRT achieves goal-driven diversity through behavior-conditioned training and implements a behavioral replay buffer in an open-ended manner. In addition, it trains multiple specialized attackers capable of generating high-quality attacks across diverse styles and risk categories. Our empirical evaluation demonstrates that QDRT generates attacks that are both more diverse and more effective against a wide range of target LLMs, including open-source models \texttt{GPT-2}, \texttt{Llama-3}, \texttt{Gemma-2}, \texttt{Qwen2.5}, and commercial models \texttt{GPT-4.1} and \texttt{GPT-5-Chat}. This work advances the field of LLM safety by providing a systematic and effective approach to automated red-teaming, ultimately supporting the responsible deployment of LLMs. Our code is available at https://github.com/lamda-bbo/QDRT.

标题：质量-多样性红队：大型语言模型高质量和多样化攻击者的自动生成
确保大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全性和稳健性是一个基本挑战，也是负责任地部署人工智能的关键前提。红队是一种系统框架，用于识别引发目标LLMs有害响应的对抗性提示，已成为重要的安全评估范式。在这一框架内，对抗性提示的多样性对全面的安全评估至关重要。然而，以往的红队方法往往通过简单的指标（如词频或句子嵌入相似性）来追求多样性，这可能无法捕捉到攻击策略的有意义变化。此外，训练单一攻击者模型的常见做法限制了对所有潜在攻击风格和风险类别的覆盖。本文介绍了质量-多样性红队（QDRT），这是一个旨在解决这些局限性的新框架。QDRT通过行为条件训练实现目标驱动的多样性，并以开放式方式实现行为重放缓冲区。此外，它训练多个专门的攻击者，能够在多样化风格和风险类别中生成高质量的攻击。我们的实证评估表明，QDRT生成的攻击在多样性和有效性方面均优于针对广泛目标LLMs的攻击，包括开源模型\texttt{GPT-2}、\texttt{Llama-3}、\texttt{Gemma-2}、\texttt{Qwen2.5}，以及商业模型\texttt{GPT-4.1}和\texttt{GPT-5-Chat}。这项工作通过提供系统和有效的自动化红队方法，推动了LLM安全领域的发展，最终支持LLMs的负责任部署。我们的代码可在https://github.com/lamda-bbo/QDRT获取。

Previous red-teaming methods have relied on simplistic metrics for diversity, such as word frequency, which fail to capture meaningful variations in attack strategies, and typically utilize a single attacker model that limits the exploration of diverse attack styles. The proposed Quality-Diversity Red-Teaming (QDRT) framework overcomes these limitations by employing behavior-conditioned training and a behavioral replay buffer, allowing for the training of multiple specialized attackers that generate high-quality and diverse attacks.
